[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/dd/Insignia_of_the_Indonesian_National_Police.svg/120px-Insignia_of_the_Indonesian_National_Police.svg.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Insignia_of_the_Indonesian_National_Police.svg)

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/16/Insignia_of_the_Indonesian_Air_Force.svg/120px-Insignia_of_the_Indonesian_Air_Force.svg.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Insignia_of_the_Indonesian_Air_Force.svg)

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/1f/Insignia_of_the_Indonesian_Army.svg/120px-Insignia_of_the_Indonesian_Army.svg.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Insignia_of_the_Indonesian_Army.svg)

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/79/Insignia_of_the_Indonesian_Navy.svg/120px-Insignia_of_the_Indonesian_Navy.svg.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Insignia_of_the_Indonesian_Navy.svg)

[Indonesian National Police](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indonesian_National_Police "Indonesian National Police"), [Indonesian Air Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indonesian_Air_Force "Indonesian Air Force"), [Indonesian Army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indonesian_Army "Indonesian Army") and [Indonesian Navy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indonesian_Navy "Indonesian Navy") mottos are _Rastra Sewakottama_, _Swa Bhuwana Paksa_, _Kartika Eka Paksi_, _Jalesveva Jayamahe_, all in the [Sanskrit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sanskrit "Sanskrit") language.

The [Sanskrit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sanskrit "Sanskrit") influence came from contacts with [India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/India "India") since ancient times. The words were either borrowed directly from India or with the intermediary of the [Old Javanese language](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Old_Javanese "Old Javanese"). Although [Hinduism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hinduism "Hinduism") and [Buddhism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Buddhism "Buddhism") are no longer the major religions of Indonesia, [Sanskrit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sanskrit "Sanskrit"), which was the language vehicle for these religions, is still held in high esteem and is comparable with the status of [Latin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Latin "Latin") in [English](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/English_language "English language") and other Western European languages. Sanskrit is also the main source for [neologisms](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neologism "Neologism"), which are usually formed from Sanskrit roots. The loanwords from Sanskrit cover many aspects of [religion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Religion "Religion"), [art](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Art "Art") and everyday life.

From [Sanskrit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sanskrit "Sanskrit") came such words as स्वर्ग _surga_ (heaven), भाषा _bahasa_ (language), काच _kaca_ (glass, mirror), राज- _raja_ (king), मनुष्य _manusia_ (mankind), चिन्ता _cinta_ (love), भूमि _bumi_ (earth), भुवन _buana_ (world), आगम _agama_ (religion), स्त्री _Istri_ (wife/woman), जय _Jaya_ (victory/victorious), पुर _Pura_ (city/temple/place) राक्षस _[Raksasa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Raksasa "Raksasa")_ (giant/monster), धर्म _[Dharma](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dharma "Dharma")_ (rule/regulations), मन्त्र _[Mantra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mantra "Mantra")_ (words/poet/spiritual prayers), क्षत्रिय _Satria_ (warrior/brave/soldier), विजय _Wijaya_ (greatly victorious/great victory), etc. [Sanskrit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sanskrit "Sanskrit") words and sentences are also used in names, titles, and mottos of the Indonesian National Police and Indonesian Armed Forces such as: _[Bhayangkara](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indonesian_National_Police "Indonesian National Police")_, _[Laksamana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Laksamana "Laksamana")_, _[Jatayu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jatayu "Jatayu")_, _[Garuda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Garuda "Garuda")_, _Dharmakerta Marga Reksyaka_, _[Jalesveva Jayamahe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indonesian_Navy "Indonesian Navy")_, _[Kartika Eka Paksi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indonesian_Army "Indonesian Army")_, _[Swa Bhuwana Paksa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indonesian_Air_Force "Indonesian Air Force")_, _[Rastra Sewakottama](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indonesian_National_Police "Indonesian National Police")_, _Yudha Siaga_, etc.

Because [Sanskrit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sanskrit "Sanskrit") has long been known in the [Indonesian archipelago](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_islands_of_Indonesia "List of islands of Indonesia"), Sanskrit loanwords, unlike those from other languages, have entered the basic vocabulary of Indonesian to such an extent that, for many, they are no longer perceived to be foreign. Therefore, one could write a short story using mostly Sanskrit-derived words. The short story below consists of approximately 80 words in Indonesian that are all derived from Sanskrit, as well as a few native [function words](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Function_word "Function word") and affixes.

### Loan words of Chinese origin

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Indonesian_language&action=edit&section=41 "Edit section: Loan words of Chinese origin")\]

See also: [List of Chinese loanwords in Indonesian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Chinese_loanwords_in_Indonesian "List of Chinese loanwords in Indonesian")

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| [![icon](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/9/99/Question_book-new.svg/60px-Question_book-new.svg.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Question_book-new.svg) | This section **does not [cite](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citing_sources "Wikipedia:Citing sources") any [sources](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Verifiability "Wikipedia:Verifiability")**. Please help [improve this section](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special:EditPage/Indonesian_language "Special:EditPage/Indonesian language") by [adding citations to reliable sources](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:Referencing_for_beginners "Help:Referencing for beginners"). Unsourced material may be challenged and [removed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Verifiability#Burden_of_evidence "Wikipedia:Verifiability")._(January 2026)__([Learn how and when to remove this message](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:Maintenance_template_removal "Help:Maintenance template removal"))_ |

The relationship with China has been going since the 7th century when Chinese merchants traded in some areas of the archipelago such as [Riau](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Riau "Riau"), [West Borneo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/West_Kalimantan "West Kalimantan"), [East Kalimantan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/East_Kalimantan "East Kalimantan"), and [North Maluku](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/North_Maluku "North Maluku"). As the kingdom of Srivijaya appeared and flourished, China opened diplomatic relations with the kingdom in order to secure trade and seafaring. In 922, Chinese travelers visited [Kahuripan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kahuripan "Kahuripan") in [East Java](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/East_Java "East Java"). Since the 11th century, hundreds of thousands of Chinese migrants left [Mainland China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mainland_China "Mainland China") and settled in many parts of Nusantara (now called Indonesia).

The [Chinese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_language "Chinese language") loanwords are usually concerned with cuisine, trade or often just things exclusively Chinese. Words of Chinese origin (presented here with accompanying [Hokkien](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hokkien "Hokkien")/ Mandarin pronunciation derivatives as well as [traditional](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Traditional_Chinese_characters "Traditional Chinese characters") and [simplified characters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Simplified_Chinese_characters "Simplified Chinese characters")) include _loteng_, (樓/層 = lóu/céng– \[upper\] floor/ level), _mie_ (麵 \> 面 Hokkien mī– noodles), _[lumpia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lumpia "Lumpia")_ (潤餅 (Hokkien = lūn-piáⁿ)– springroll), _cawan_ (茶碗 cháwǎn– teacup), _teko_ (茶壺 \> 茶壶 = cháhú \[Mandarin\], teh-ko \[Hokkien\] = teapot), 苦力 kuli (= 苦 khu (hard) and 力 li (energy) – coolie) and even the widely used slang terms _gua_ and _lu_ (from the Hokkien 'goa' 我 and 'lu/li' 汝– meaning 'I/ me' and 'you').

### Loan words of Arabic origin

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Indonesian_language&action=edit&section=42 "Edit section: Loan words of Arabic origin")\]

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/4/48/Jakarta_Indonesia_Istiqlal-Mosque-03.jpg/250px-Jakarta_Indonesia_Istiqlal-Mosque-03.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Jakarta_Indonesia_Istiqlal-Mosque-03.jpg) The word _masjid_ (mosque) in Indonesian derived from [Arabic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arabic_language "Arabic language") word __masjid__ (مسجد); the Indonesian word _istiqlal_ or _istiklal_ (independence, freedom) is similarly derived from the Arabic __istiqlāl__ (استقلال).

Many Arabic words were brought and spread by merchants from [Arab Peninsula](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arabian_Peninsula "Arabian Peninsula") like [Arabian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arabs "Arabs"), [Persian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Persians "Persians"), and from the western part of India, [Gujarat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gujarat "Gujarat") where many Muslims lived.[\[136\]](#ref-catdir.loc.gov) As a result, many Indonesian words come from the Arabic language. Especially since the late 12th century, Old Malay was heavily influenced by the language and produced many great literary works such as [Syair](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syair "Syair"), [Babad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javanese_historical_texts "Javanese historical texts"), [Hikayat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hikayat "Hikayat"), and Suluk. This century is known as _The Golden Age of Indonesian Literature_.[\[136\]](#ref-catdir.loc.gov)

Many loanwords from [Arabic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arabic "Arabic") are mainly concerned with religion, in particular with [Islam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islam "Islam"), and by extension, with greetings such as the word, "selamat" (from [Arabic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arabic_language "Arabic language"): سلامة __salāma__ = health, soundness)[\[137\]](#ref-138) means "safe" or "lucky". Words of [Arabic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arabic "Arabic") origin include _dunia_ (from [Arabic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arabic_language "Arabic language"): دنيا __dunyā__ = the present world), names of days (except _Minggu_), such as _Sabtu_ (from [Arabic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arabic_language "Arabic language"): سبت __sabt-u__ = Saturday), _iklan_ (آعلان __iʻlan__ = advertisement), _kabar_ (خبر __khabar__ = news), _Kursi_ (كرسي __kursī__ = a chair), _ijazah_ (إجازة __ijāza__ = 'permission', certificate of authority, e.g. a school diploma certificate), _kitab_ (كتاب __kitāb__ = book), _tertib_ (ترتيب __tartīb__ = order/arrangement) and _kamus_ (قاموس __qāmūs__ = dictionary). [Allah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Allah "Allah") ( [Arabic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arabic_language "Arabic language"): الله), as is mostly the case for [Arabic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Varieties_of_Arabic "Varieties of Arabic") speakers, this is the word for [God](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/God "God") even in [Christian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christianity "Christianity") [Bible](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bible "Bible") translations. Many early Bible translators, when they came across some unusual [Hebrew](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hebrew_language "Hebrew language") words or proper names, used the Arabic cognates. In the newer translations this practice is discontinued. They now turn to [Greek](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greek_language "Greek language") names or use the original Hebrew Word. For example, the name [Jesus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jesus "Jesus") was initially translated as [' _Isa_](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jesus_in_Islam "Jesus in Islam") ( [Arabic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arabic_language "Arabic language"): عيسى), but is now spelt as _Yesus_. Several ecclesiastical terms derived from Arabic still exist in Indonesian language. Indonesian word for _[bishop](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bishop "Bishop")_ is _uskup_ (from [Arabic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arabic_language "Arabic language"): أسقف __usquf__ = bishop). This in turn makes the Indonesian term for [archbishop](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Archbishop "Archbishop") _uskup agung_ (lit.'great bishop'), which is combining the Arabic word with an Old Javanese word. The term _imam_ (from [Arabic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arabic_language "Arabic language"): إمام __imām__ = leader, prayer leader) is used to translate a Catholic [priest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Priest "Priest"), beside its more common association with an Islamic prayer leader. Some Protestant denominations refer to their congregation _jemaat_ (from [Arabic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arabic_language "Arabic language"): جماعة __jamāʻa__ = group, a community). Even the name of the [Bible](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bible "Bible") in Indonesian translation is _Alkitab_ (from [Arabic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arabic_language "Arabic language"): الكتاب __al-kitāb__ = the book), which literally means "the Book".

### Loan words of Portuguese origin

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Indonesian_language&action=edit&section=43 "Edit section: Loan words of Portuguese origin")\]

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/93/Front_gate_Ganjuran.jpg/250px-Front_gate_Ganjuran.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Front_gate_Ganjuran.jpg) Indonesian word " _[Gereja](https://en.wiktionary.org/wiki/gereja "wikt:gereja")_" (Church) is derived from Portuguese " _[Igreja](https://en.wiktionary.org/wiki/igreja "wikt:igreja")_", while the word " _[kudus](https://en.wiktionary.org/wiki/kudus "wiktionary:kudus")_" (holy; sacred) is derived from Arabic " _[قدس](https://en.wiktionary.org/wiki/%D9%82%D8%AF%D8%B3#Arabic "wiktionary:قدس") (qudus)_". The sign reads: [" _Gereja & Candi Hati Kudus Tuhan Yesus Ganjuran Keuskupan Agung Semarang_"](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ganjuran_Church "Ganjuran Church") (The Church and [Temple](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Temple "Temple") of the [Sacred Heart of Jesus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sacred_Heart "Sacred Heart") Ganjuran [Archdiocese of Semarang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roman_Catholic_Archdiocese_of_Semarang "Roman Catholic Archdiocese of Semarang")).

Alongside [Malay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Malay_language "Malay language"), [Portuguese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Portuguese_language "Portuguese language") was the _[lingua franca](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lingua_franca "Lingua franca")_ for trade throughout the archipelago from the sixteenth century through to the early nineteenth century. The Portuguese were among the first westerners to sail eastwards to the " [Spice Islands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maluku_Islands "Maluku Islands")". Loanwords from [Portuguese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Portuguese_language "Portuguese language") were mainly connected with articles that the early European traders and explorers brought to Southeast Asia. Indonesian words derived from Portuguese include _meja_ (from _mesa_ = table), _bangku_ (from _banco_ = bench), _lemari/almari_ (from _armário_ = closet), _boneka_ (from _boneca_ = doll), _jendela_ (from _janela_ = window), _gereja_ (from _igreja_ = church), _misa_ (from _missa_ = mass), _Natal_ (from _Natal_ = Christmas), _Paskah_ (from _Páscoa_ = Easter), _pesta_ (from _festa_ = party), _dansa_ (from _dança_ = dance), _pesiar_ (from _passear_ = cruise), _bendera_ (from _bandeira_ = flag), _sepatu_ (from _sapato_ = shoes), _garpu_ (from _garfo_ = fork), _kemeja_ (from _camisa_ = shirt), _kereta_ (from _carreta_ = chariot), _pompa_ (from _bomba hidráulica_ = pump), _pigura_ (from _figura_ = picture), _roda_ (from _roda_ = wheel), _nona_ (from _dona_ = young woman), _sekolah_ (from _escola_ = school), _lentera_ (from _lanterna_ = lantern), _paderi_ (from _padre_ = priest), _Santo, Santa_ (from _Santo, Santa_ = Saint), _puisi_ (from _poesia_ = poetry), _keju_ (from _queijo_ = cheese), _mentega_ (from _manteiga_ = butter), _serdadu_ (from _soldado_ = soldier), _meski_ (from _mas que_ = although), _kamar_ (from _câmara_ = room), _laguna_ (from _laguna_ = lagoon), _lelang_ (from _leilão_ = auction), _persero_ (from _parceiro_ = company), _markisa_ (from _maracujá_ = passion fruit), _limau_ (from _limão_ = lemon), _kartu_ (from _cartão_ = card), _Inggris_ (from _inglês_ = English), _Sabtu_ (from _sábado_ = Saturday), _Minggu_ (from _domingo_ = Sunday), etc.[\[138\]](#ref-RICKLEFSp24)

### Loan words of Dutch origin

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Indonesian_language&action=edit&section=44 "Edit section: Loan words of Dutch origin")\]

See also: [List of Dutch loanwords in Indonesian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Dutch_loanwords_in_Indonesian "List of Dutch loanwords in Indonesian")

[![Crowd at a busy street intersection. There are horse-drawn carriages in the foreground while a three-story building (with the sign "Kam Leng") and a single-story building (with the sign "Chunghua Bioscoop") stand in the background on adjacent corners of the intersection.](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f1/COLLECTIE_TROPENMUSEUM_Glodok_wijk_te_Djakarta_TMnr_10014951.jpg/250px-COLLECTIE_TROPENMUSEUM_Glodok_wijk_te_Djakarta_TMnr_10014951.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:COLLECTIE_TROPENMUSEUM_Glodok_wijk_te_Djakarta_TMnr_10014951.jpg) The Indonesian word of _bioskop_ is derived from [Dutch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dutch_language "Dutch language") _bioscoop_ ( [movie theater](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Movie_theater "Movie theater")).

The former colonial power, the [Netherlands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Netherlands "Netherlands"), left a sizeable amount of vocabulary that can be seen in words such as _polisi_ (from _politie_ = police), _kualitas_ (from _kwaliteit_ = quality), _aktual_ (from _actueel_ = current), _rokok_ (from _roken_ = smoking cigarettes), _korupsi_ (from _corruptie_ = corruption), _kantor_ (from _kantoor_ = office), _resleting_ (from _ritssluiting_ = zipper), _pelopor_ (from _voorloper_ = frontrunner), _persneling_ (from _versnelling_ = transmission gear), _setrum_ (from _stroom_ = electricity current), _maskapai_ (from _maatschappij_ = company), _apotek_ (from _apotheek_ = pharmacy), _handuk_ (from _handdoek_ = towel), _setrika_ (from _strijkijzer_ = clothes iron), _bioskop_ (from _bioscoop_ = [movie theater](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Movie_theater "Movie theater")), _spanduk_ (from _spandoeken_ = banner), _korsleting_ (from _kortsluiting_ = short circuit), _om_ (from _oom_ = uncle), _tante_ (from _tante_ = aunt), _traktir_ (from _trakteer_ = treat) and _gratis_ (from _gratis_ = free).

These [Dutch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dutch_language "Dutch language") loanwords, and many other non-Ibero-Romance, European language loanwords that came via Dutch, cover all aspects of life. Some Dutch loanwords, having clusters of several consonants, pose difficulties to speakers of Indonesian. This problem is usually solved by insertion of the [schwa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mid_central_vowel "Mid central vowel"). For example, Dutch _schroef_\[ˈsxruf\] \> _sekrup_\[səˈkrup\] (screw (n.)). One scholar argues that 25% of Indonesian words are inspired by the Dutch language.[\[139\]](#ref-mai)

Before the standardization of the language, many Indonesian words follow standard Dutch alphabet and pronunciation such as "oe" for vowel "u" or "dj" for consonant "j" \[dʒ\]. As a result, Malay words are written with that orthography such as: _passer_ for the word _Pasar_ or _djalan_ for the word _jalan_, older Indonesian generation tend to have their name written in such order as well.

### Loan words of English origin

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Indonesian_language&action=edit&section=45 "Edit section: Loan words of English origin")\]

Many English words were incorporated into Indonesian through [globalization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Globalization "Globalization"). Many Indonesians, however, mistake words already adopted from Dutch as words borrowed from English. Indonesian adopts English words with standardization.[\[140\]](#ref-141) For example: _a **ks** esor **i**_ from a **cc** essor **y**.[\[141\]](#ref-142)[\[142\]](#ref-lib.ui.ac.id) However, there are several words that directly borrowed without standardization that have same meanings in English such as: _bus_, _data_, _domain_, _detail_, _internet_, _film_, _golf_, _lift_, _monitor_, _radio_, _radar_, _unit_, _safari_, _sonar_, _video_, and _riil as real_.[\[142\]](#ref-lib.ui.ac.id)

### Other loan words

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Indonesian_language&action=edit&section=46 "Edit section: Other loan words")\]

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| [![icon](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/9/99/Question_book-new.svg/60px-Question_book-new.svg.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Question_book-new.svg) | This section **does not [cite](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citing_sources "Wikipedia:Citing sources") any [sources](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Verifiability "Wikipedia:Verifiability")**. Please help [improve this section](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special:EditPage/Indonesian_language "Special:EditPage/Indonesian language") by [adding citations to reliable sources](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:Referencing_for_beginners "Help:Referencing for beginners"). Unsourced material may be challenged and [removed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Verifiability#Burden_of_evidence "Wikipedia:Verifiability")._(January 2026)__([Learn how and when to remove this message](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:Maintenance_template_removal "Help:Maintenance template removal"))_ |

Modern Indonesian draws many of its words from foreign sources; there are many [synonyms](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Synonym "Synonym"). For example, Indonesian has three words for "book", i.e. _pustaka_ (from Sanskrit), _kitab_ (from Arabic) and _buku_ (from Dutch _boek_); however, each has a slightly different meaning. A _pustaka_ is often connected with ancient wisdom or sometimes with esoteric knowledge. A derived form, _perpustakaan_ means a library. A _kitab_ is usually a religious scripture or a book containing moral guidance. The Indonesian words for the [Bible](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bible "Bible") and [Gospel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gospel "Gospel") are _Alkitab_ and _Injil_, both directly derived from Arabic. The book containing the penal code is also called the _kitab_. _Buku_ is the most common word for books.

There are direct borrowings from various other languages of the world, such as _karaoke_ (from カラオケ) from [Japanese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Japanese_language "Japanese language"), and _ebi_ (from えび) which means dried shrimp. Many words that originally are adopted through the Dutch language today however often are mistaken as English due to the similarity in the Germanic nature of both languages. In some cases the words are replaced by English language through globalization: although the word _arbei_ ( [Dutch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dutch_language "Dutch language"): _aardbei_) still literally means strawberry in Indonesian, today the usage of the word _stroberi_ is more common. [Greek](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greek_language "Greek language") words such as _demokrasi_ (from δημοκρατία_dēmokratía_), _filosofi_, _filsafat_ (both from φιλοσοφία_philosophia_), _mitos_ (from μῦθος_mythos_) came through Dutch, Arabic and Portuguese respectively.

It is notable that some of the loanwords that exist in both Indonesian and Malaysian languages are different in spelling and pronunciation mainly due to how they derived their origins: Malaysian utilises words that reflect the [English](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/English_language "English language") usage (as used by its former colonial power, the [British](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Kingdom "United Kingdom")), while Indonesian uses a [Latinate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Latin "Latin") form (e.g. _aktiviti_ (Malaysian) vs. _aktivitas_ (Indonesian), _universiti_ (Malaysian) vs. _universitas_ (Indonesian)).

### Acronyms and portmanteau

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Indonesian_language&action=edit&section=47 "Edit section: Acronyms and portmanteau")\]

Since the time of the independence of Indonesia, Indonesian has seen a surge of [neologisms](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neologism "Neologism") which are formed as [acronyms](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Acronym "Acronym") (less commonly also [initialisms](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Initialism "Initialism")) or [blend words](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Portmanteau "Portmanteau").

Common acronyms are _ABRI_ (pronounced[\[ˈabri\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:IPA/Malay "Help:IPA/Malay"), from _Angkatan Bersenjata Republik Indonesia_ ' [Indonesian National Armed Forces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indonesian_National_Armed_Forces "Indonesian National Armed Forces")'), _SIM_ (pronounced[\[sim\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:IPA/Malay "Help:IPA/Malay"), from _surat izin mengemudi_ 'driving licence'), _SARA_ (pronounced[\[ˈsara\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:IPA/Malay "Help:IPA/Malay"), from _suku, agama, ras, antargolongan_ 'ethnic group, religion, race, inter-group \[matters\]', used when referring to the background of intercommunal conflicts), _HAM_ (pronounced[\[ham\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:IPA/Malay "Help:IPA/Malay"), from _hak asasi manusia_ 'human rights').

Blend words/portmanteau are very common in Indonesian, and have become a productive tool of word formation in both formal and colloquial Indonesian. Examples from official usage include departments and officeholders (e.g. _Menlu_ < _Mentri Luar Negeri_ 'Foreign Minister', _Kapolda_ < _Kepala kepolisian daerah_ 'Head of Regional Police') or names of provinces and districts (_Sulut_ < _Sulawesi Utara_ 'North Sulawesi', _Jabar_ < _Jawa Barat_ 'West Java'). Other commonly used portmanteau include _puskesmas_ < _pusat kesehatan masyarakat_ 'community health center', _sembako_ < _sembilan bahan pokok_ 'basic commodities' (lit.'nine basic commodities').[\[11\]](#ref-The_Indonesian_Language_2004):145–149

## Literature

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Indonesian_language&action=edit&section=48 "Edit section: Literature")\]

Main article: [Indonesian literature](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indonesian_literature "Indonesian literature")

Indonesia hosts a variety of traditional verbal arts such as [poetry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Poetry "Poetry"), [historical narratives](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Narrative_history "Narrative history"), [romances](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romance_film "Romance film"), and [drama](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Drama "Drama"), which are expressed in local languages, but modern genres are expressed mainly in Indonesian.[\[14\]](#ref-hawaii.edu) Some of the classic Indonesian stories include _[Sitti Nurbaya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sitti_Nurbaya "Sitti Nurbaya")_ by [Marah Rusli](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marah_Roesli "Marah Roesli"), _[Azab dan Sengsara](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Azab_dan_Sengsara "Azab dan Sengsara")_ by [Merari Siregar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Merari_Siregar "Merari Siregar"), and _[Sengsara Membawa Nikmat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sengsara_Membawa_Nikmat "Sengsara Membawa Nikmat")_ by [Tulis Sutan Sati](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tulis_Sutan_Sati "Tulis Sutan Sati").[\[143\]](#ref-144)[\[144\]](#ref-145) Modern literature like [novels](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Novel "Novel"), [short stories](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Short_story "Short story"), stage plays, and [free-form poetry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Free_verse "Free verse") has developed since the late years of the 19th century and has produced figures such as [novelist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Novelist "Novelist") [Pramoedya Ananta Toer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pramoedya_Ananta_Toer "Pramoedya Ananta Toer"), [dramatist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Playwright "Playwright") [W.S. Rendra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rendra "Rendra"), poet [Chairil Anwar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chairil_Anwar "Chairil Anwar"), and [cinematographer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cinematographer "Cinematographer") [Garin Nugroho](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Garin_Nugroho "Garin Nugroho").[\[145\]](#ref-146) Indonesia's classic novels themselves offer insight into the [local culture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Culture_of_Indonesia "Culture of Indonesia") and traditions and the historical background before and immediately after the country gained independence. One notable example is _Shackles_, which was written by [Armijn Pane](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Armijn_Pane "Armijn Pane") in 1940. Originally titled _[Belenggu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Belenggu "Belenggu")_, it has been translated into many languages, including [English](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/English_language "English language") and [German](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/German_language "German language").[\[146\]](#ref-147)

## As speakers of other languages

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Indonesian_language&action=edit&section=49 "Edit section: As speakers of other languages")\]

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/cb/BIPA_photograph.jpg/250px-BIPA_photograph.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:BIPA_photograph.jpg) _BIPA (_Bahasa Indonesia untuk Penutur Asing_)_ book, which helps foreigners learn the Indonesian language

Over the past few years, interest in learning Indonesian has grown among non-Indonesians.[\[147\]](#ref-148) Various universities have started to offer courses that emphasise the teaching of the language to non-Indonesians. In addition to national universities, private institutions have also started to offer courses, like the Indonesia Australia Language Foundation and the _Lembaga Indonesia Amerika_. As early as 1988, teachers of the language have expressed the importance of a standardized _Bahasa Indonesia bagi Penutur Asing_ (also called **BIPA**, literally _Indonesian Language for Foreign Speaker_) materials (mostly books), and this need became more evident during the 4th International Congress on the Teaching of Indonesian to Speakers of Other Languages held in 2001.[\[148\]](#ref-149)

Since 2013, the Indonesian embassy in the Philippines has given basic Indonesian language courses to 16 batches of Filipino students, as well as training to members of the [Armed Forces of the Philippines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Armed_Forces_of_the_Philippines "Armed Forces of the Philippines"). In an interview, [Department of Education](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Department_of_Education_(Philippines) "Department of Education (Philippines)") Secretary [Armin Luistro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Armin_Luistro "Armin Luistro")[\[149\]](#ref-150) said that the country's government should promote Indonesian or Malay, which are related to Filipino. Thus, the possibility of offering it as an optional subject in public schools is being studied.

The Indonesian embassy in Washington, D.C., United States, also began offering free Indonesian language courses at the beginner and intermediate level.[\[150\]](#ref-151)

| See you later! | _Sampai jumpa lagi!_ | [\[ˈsampai̯ˈdʒumpaˈlagi\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:IPA/Malay "Help:IPA/Malay") |

| Where? | _Di mana?_ | [\[diˈmana\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:IPA/Malay "Help:IPA/Malay") |
| Why? | _Mengapa?_ (standard) or _kenapa?_ (colloquial) | [\[mə'ŋapa\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:IPA/Malay "Help:IPA/Malay") or [\[kə'napa\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:IPA/Malay "Help:IPA/Malay") |
| How? | _Bagaimana?_ | [\[baɡai̯'mana\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:IPA/Malay "Help:IPA/Malay") |
| How much? | _Berapa?_ | [\[bə'rapa\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:IPA/Malay "Help:IPA/Malay") |
| What is your name? | _Nama Anda siapa_? | [\[ˈnamaˈʔandasiˈapa\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:IPA/Malay "Help:IPA/Malay") |
| My name is... | _Nama saya..._ | [\[ˈnamaˈsaja\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:IPA/Malay "Help:IPA/Malay") |
| Do you know? | _Apakah Anda tahu?_ | [\[aˈpakahˈʔandaˈtahu\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:IPA/Malay "Help:IPA/Malay") |
| Yes, I know / No, I do not know | _Ya, saya tahu / Tidak, saya tidak tahu_ | [\[ˈjaˈsajaˈtahu\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:IPA/Malay "Help:IPA/Malay") / [\[ˈtidaʔˈsajaˈtidaʔˈtahu\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:IPA/Malay "Help:IPA/Malay") |
| Can you speak Indonesian? | _Bisakah Anda berbicara bahasa Indonesia?_ | [\[biˈsakahˈʔandabərbiˈtʃarabaˈhasaʔindoˈnesi̯a\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:IPA/Malay "Help:IPA/Malay") |
| Yes, I can speak Indonesian / No, I can not speak Indonesian | _Ya, saya bisa berbicara bahasa Indonesia / Tidak, saya tidak bisa berbicara bahasa indonesia_ | [\[ˈjaˈsajaˈbisabərbiˈtʃarabaˈhasaʔindoˈnesi̯a\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:IPA/Malay "Help:IPA/Malay") / [\[ˈtidaʔˈsajaˈtidaʔˈbisabərbiˈtʃarabaˈhasaʔindoˈnesi̯a\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:IPA/Malay "Help:IPA/Malay") |
| What time is it now? | _Pukul berapa sekarang?_ | [\[ˈpukulbə'rapasəˈkaraŋ\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:IPA/Malay "Help:IPA/Malay") |
| It is 5.00 o'clock | _Sekarang pukul 5.00_ | [\[səˈkaraŋˈpukulˈlima\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:IPA/Malay "Help:IPA/Malay") |
| When will you go to the party? | _Kapan Anda akan pergi ke pesta itu?_ | [\[ˈkapanˈʔandaˈʔakanpər'gikeˈpestaˈʔitu\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:IPA/Malay "Help:IPA/Malay") |
| Soon | _Nanti_ | [\[ˈnanti\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:IPA/Malay "Help:IPA/Malay") |
| Today | _Hari ini_ | [\[ˈhariˈʔini\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:IPA/Malay "Help:IPA/Malay") |
| Tomorrow | _Besok_ | [\[ˈbesok\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:IPA/Malay "Help:IPA/Malay") |
| The day after tomorrow | _Lusa_ | [\[ˈlusa\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:IPA/Malay "Help:IPA/Malay") |
| Yesterday | _Kemarin_ | [\[kə'marin\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:IPA/Malay "Help:IPA/Malay") |
| Congratulations! | _Selamat!_ | [\[sə'lamat\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:IPA/Malay "Help:IPA/Malay") |
| Happy New Year! | _Selamat Tahun Baru!_ | [\[sə'lamatˈtahunˈbaru\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:IPA/Malay "Help:IPA/Malay") |
| Merry Christmas! | _Selamat Natal!_ | [\[sə'lamatˈnatal\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:IPA/Malay "Help:IPA/Malay") |
| Please | _Mohon_ or _tolong_ | [\[ˈmohon\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:IPA/Malay "Help:IPA/Malay") or [\[ˈtoloŋ\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:IPA/Malay "Help:IPA/Malay") |
| Stop! | _Berhenti!_ | [\[bər'henti\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:IPA/Malay "Help:IPA/Malay") |
| I am happy | _Saya senang_ | [\[ˈsajasə'naŋ\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:IPA/Malay "Help:IPA/Malay") |
| I understand | _Saya mengerti_ | [\[ˈsajaˈməŋərti\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:IPA/Malay "Help:IPA/Malay") |
| Help! | _Tolong!_ | [\[ˈtoloŋ\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:IPA/Malay "Help:IPA/Malay") |
| I need help | _Saya memerlukan bantuan_ | [\[ˈsajaməmərˈlukanban'tuan\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:IPA/Malay "Help:IPA/Malay") |
| Can you help me? | _Bisakah Anda menolong saya?_ | [\[biˈsakahˈʔandamə'noloŋˈsaja\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:IPA/Malay "Help:IPA/Malay") |
| Can I help you? / Do you need help? | _Dapatkah saya membantu Anda? / Apakah Anda membutuhkan bantuan?_ | [\[da'patkahˈsajaməm'bantuˈʔanda\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:IPA/Malay "Help:IPA/Malay") / [\[aˈpakahˈʔandaməmbuˈtuhkanbanˈtuan\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:IPA/Malay "Help:IPA/Malay") |
| May I borrow your eraser? | _Bolehkah saya meminjam penghapus Anda?_ | [\[boˈlehkahˈsajamə'mindʒampəŋ'hapusˈʔanda\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:IPA/Malay "Help:IPA/Malay") |
| With my pleasure | _Dengan senang hati_ | [\[dəˈŋansə'naŋˈhati\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:IPA/Malay "Help:IPA/Malay") |
| Welcome | _Selamat datang_ | [\[sə'lamatˈdataŋ\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:IPA/Malay "Help:IPA/Malay") |
| Welcome to Indonesia | _Selamat datang di Indonesia_ | [\[sə'lamatˈdataŋdiʔindoˈnesi̯a\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:IPA/Malay "Help:IPA/Malay") |
| I agree / I disagree | _Saya setuju / Saya tidak setuju_ | [\[ˈsajasə'tudʒu\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:IPA/Malay "Help:IPA/Malay") / [\[ˈsajaˈtidaʔsə'tudʒu\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:IPA/Malay "Help:IPA/Malay") |
| I understand / I do not understand | _Saya mengerti / Saya tidak mengerti_ | [\[ˈsajaˈməŋərti\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:IPA/Malay "Help:IPA/Malay") / [\[ˈsajaˈtidaʔˈməŋərti\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:IPA/Malay "Help:IPA/Malay") |
| I am hungry | _Saya lapar_ | [\[ˈsajaˈlapar\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:IPA/Malay "Help:IPA/Malay") |
| I am thirsty | _Saya haus_ | [\[ˈsajaˈhaus\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:IPA/Malay "Help:IPA/Malay") |
| I am sick | _Saya sakit_ | [\[ˈsajaˈsakit\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:IPA/Malay "Help:IPA/Malay") |
| Get well soon | _Semoga cepat sembuh_ | [\[sə'mogatʃə'patsəmˈbuh\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:IPA/Malay "Help:IPA/Malay") |
| Next lesson | _Pelajaran selanjutnya_ | [\[pə'ladʒaransə'landʒutɲa\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:IPA/Malay "Help:IPA/Malay") |

### Example

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Indonesian_language&action=edit&section=58 "Edit section: Example")\]

The following texts are excerpts from the official translations of the [Universal Declaration of Human Rights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Universal_Declaration_of_Human_Rights "Universal Declaration of Human Rights") in Indonesian along with the original declaration in English.[\[154\]](#ref-155)[\[155\]](#ref-156)

English: _**Universal Declaration of Human Rights**_

All human beings are born free and equal in dignity and rights. They are endowed with reason and conscience and should act towards one another in a spirit of brotherhood.

Indonesian: _**Pernyataan Umum tentang Hak Asasi Manusia**_

Semua orang dilahirkan merdeka dan mempunyai martabat dan hak-hak yang sama. Mereka dikaruniai akal dan hati nurani dan hendaknya bergaul satu sama lain dalam semangat persaudaraan.
